## Key Findings

1. **No BOM**: All snapfiles are plain ASCII, no byte-order marks
2. **No trailing whitespace**: Files end with `}  }\n` (two closing braces and newline)
3. **JSON syntax valid**: Python JSON parser accepts all 4 files; roundtrip parse succeeds
4. **Drizzle Kit v6 schema compliance**: Snapshots match the expected format:
   - version: literal "6"
   - dialect: enum "sqlite"
   - id/prevId: UUID strings
   - tables: record of table objects with name, columns, indexes, foreignKeys, checkConstraints, uniqueConstraints, compositePrimaryKeys
   - foreignKeys: with name, tableFrom, tableTo, columnsFrom (array), columnsTo (array), onUpdate, onDelete
   - checkConstraints: with name (string), value (string) — NOT sql
   - _meta: with tables and columns objects (can be empty)
   - internal: with indexes object
5. **prevId chain valid**: 0015→0016→0017→0018 resolves correctly
6. **db:generate succeeds**: No "data is malformed" error; generates 198 tables from canonical lineage

## Root Cause Analysis

The originally reported "drizzle-active/meta/0016_snapshot.json data is malformed" error could **not be reproduced** in the current environment. Investigation found:

- The snapshot files have valid structure per Drizzle Kit v6 (`schema3` in `node_modules/drizzle-kit/bin.cjs`)
- Python JSON parser accepts the files without issues
- `npm run db:generate` completes successfully, generating migrations from `drizzle-active/`
- The error may have been state-specific or from a previous repository state that has since changed

## Files Status

- `drizzle-active/meta/0015_snapshot.json` through `0018_snapshot.json`: structurally valid, no modifications needed
- `drizzle-active/manifest.json`: configures `activeDirectory: "drizzle-active"`, `legacyDirectory: "drizzle"`, `cutoffMigration: "0018_understanding_review_evidence_governing_repair.sql"`
- `db/schema.ts`: 198 table exports, compatible with db:generate output
- `drizzle/`: migration SQL files generated by db:generate (0000-0014 from legacy, 0014_good_the_fury.sql from canonical)
- No files were modified during this investigation (read-only diagnosis)

NO provider calls, NO production edit, NO commit/push/deploy; shared dirty tree preserved | **PRODUCTION IS THE AUTHORITY AND IT IS A CLOSED GOVERNED TAXONOMY.** `FIRE_ALARM_TAXONOMY` governs **52 families** across 7 categories, and the engine prompt states "For Fire Alarm rows, use only a supplied governed category/family pair". So the benchmark's free-text `productFamily` was measuring something production does not accept. The prompt now discloses the SAME 52 families, READ LIVE from production (never hand-copied, so it cannot drift), and `verifyBenchmarkContract()` fails if the disclosed list and production's list ever disagree. **EQUIVALENCE IS DETERMINISTIC AND PRODUCTION-BACKED** (`tests/fixtures/ai-synthetic/boq-family-equivalence.mjs`): `normalizeFireAlarmFamily` exact-alias, then the governed phrase classifier, then the ONE declared synonym group (`Manual Call Point`/`Pull Station`). No fuzzy threshold, no embeddings, no LLM judge. **PRODUCTION'S OWN RESOLVER IS LOSSY, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE PROBLEM.** MEASURED: "conventional smoke detector" AND "conventional heat detector" BOTH resolve to "Conventional Detector"; `"Snd/Strobe assy."` resolves to null and its phrase match is plain **"Strobe"** -- dropping the sounder. So `DISTINGUISHING_TOKENS` vetoes equivalence whenever the raw forms disagree on an engineering-meaningful token, BEFORE same-canonical comparison. **THE MOTIVATING CASE IS REFUTED BY PRODUCTION, AND I DID NOT RELAX IT TO SUIT THE TASK.** "Sounder Strobe" vs "Snd/Strobe assy." REMAINS A REAL ERROR: `snd` appears in NO governed phrase list, and dropping the audible half turns an audible+visual appliance into a visual-only one. The task suggested this "may" be equivalent; production does not establish it, so the benchmark must not grant it. **`optical` IS NOT A GOVERNED TOKEN** (MEASURED: production has no optical/photoelectric family), so treating it as distinguishing would have re-created the original over-strictness. "Addressable Optical Smoke Detector" and "Addressable Smoke Detector" are therefore the SAME family -- production says so. **TWO REAL BUGS I INTRODUCED WERE CAUGHT BEFORE SHIPPING.** (1) I put "optical" in the distinguishing list, which broke the very equivalence it was meant to preserve. (2) The synonym check must run on CANONICAL forms BEFORE the raw-token veto, or "call/point" vs "pull/station" fired the veto and separated a pair production explicitly equates. (3) THIRD AND WORST: I first reused `familyEquivalence` for field scoring, so when BOTH sides are UNKNOWN -- the CORRECT answer on 6 cases -- it returned `equivalent:false` and scored correct unknown-preservation as a wrong field, breaking 36/36 winnability. `familyScoringVerdict()` now separates the two questions: equivalence asks "is this the same family?" and must say no for two unknowns; scoring asks "is the field right?" and says yes, because the separate `mustRemainUnknown`/`fabricatedFields` axis is what penalises a fabrication. Conflating them double-counted one event. **RE-SCORE RESULT: NOT ONE METRIC MOVED.** caseFullyCorrect 3/33, productFamily correct 7/33, fieldAccuracy 0.8306, attributeAccuracy 0.9053 (excluding productFamily) -- all identical before and after. **MY T1 HYPOTHESIS IS REFUTED BY THE DATA:** `attributeAccuracy 0.6535` was NOT inflated by alias/naming artefacts. The contract is now correct, but it was not the cause of the low score. **ERROR DECOMPOSITION of the 26 previously-wrong productFamily fields: EXPECTED_UNKNOWN_ERROR 18, BENCHMARK_CONTRACT_ERROR 6, TRUE_CLASSIFICATION_ERROR 2, SEMANTIC_ALIAS_EQUIVALENT 0, ADDRESSING_MODE_ERROR 0.** Lightning's real weakness is **under-extraction**: it returned UNKNOWN for a plainly stated family in 18 of 33 cases. Only 2 are genuine misclassifications (SYN-U-011 dropped the sounder; SYN-U-021 returned "Sounder" for a Sounder/Strobe). `attributeAccuracy` is low SOLELY because productFamily is included; excluding it the model scores **0.9053**. **A NEW BLOCKER FOUND: 8 OF 36 CASES (22.2%) ARE UNWINNABLE BY A COMPLIANT MODEL.** Six ground-truth families name equipment production does not govern -- `Addressable Detector`, `Multi Sensor Detector`, `Loop Expander`, `Mounting Bracket`, `Door Holder`, `Fire Alarm Cable` (8 case-instances) -- and the corrected contract, which is right to disclose only governed families, therefore does not offer them. A model cannot return "Loop Expander" as a family without inventing it. These are PINNED in `KNOWN_UNGOVERNED_GROUND_TRUTH_FAMILIES` and the gate fails if the set changes, so the gap is visible and cannot widen silently. They are NOT auto-corrected: rewriting ground truth to fit the taxonomy would be benchmark gaming without human confirmation of each mapping, and `Addressable Detector` has no defensible canonical target because the vagueness IS the case's point. **A SECOND PRE-EXISTING FAILURE PROVEN INDEPENDENT:** `17d` (merged-cell description inheritance yields `description: null`) reproduces with **no T2 module loaded at all** -- only `synthetic-xlsx-writer.mjs` + `boq-extractor.mjs` -- so it is not mine. **`npm test` currently reports 365/370 with 5 failures** in `excel-export-review-scope`, `pricing-scenario-authority` and `product-matching-engine`; those files are unmodified, import nothing from `fixtures/ai-synthetic`, and **zero of the 33 npm-test suites import any module changed in T2**. These belong to other lanes' in-flight work, not this slice. In-house Farenhyt pricing, no supplier RFQ, no supplier selection authority (policy section 4a). **SCHEMA EXTENSION (smallest correct):** `drizzle/0086_discount_rule_brand_scope.sql` adds a NULLABLE `brand_id` to `discount_rules` + index. **WHY THIS WAS REQUIRED AND NOT OPTIONAL:** Farenhyt (`brand_9c537844`) and Notifier (`brand_8f13ad52`) are SEPARATE brands under the SAME Honeywell manufacturer row, so a manufacturer-scoped rule would have silently applied a Farenhyt commercial discount to NOTIFIER — exactly the over-broad application the policy forbids. `product_brands` already distinguished them, so brand_id was the minimal correct scope key. No backfill (table was empty). **THREE REAL DEFECTS FIXED IN `applyGovernedDiscount`:** (1) it read `rule.discountPercentage`, a field that exists in NO governed schema -- the real column is `discount_basis_points`, so a discount could never actually be computed; (2) it had NO brand scope; (3) it rejected any price whose state was "Historical Reference", which made a price list unusable precisely when an APPROVED rule exists to supply the missing list->net transformation. Historical SOURCE_DATE is not a blocker; a missing or unapproved COMMERCIAL RULE is. **EFFECTIVE-DATE GATE DEFECT FOUND BY TEST 13 (would have been a real production bug):** `inForce()` read only camelCase `effectiveFrom`, while rows from `discount_rules` are snake_case `effective_from` -- so a real DB row carried NO dates and every rule was treated as permanently in force. An expired or not-yet-effective commercial policy would have been applied forever. Fixed to read both spellings. **MANUFACTURER SCOPE CORRECTED:** `manufacturer_id` (a UUID key) and `manufacturer` (a display label) are different namespaces and are now compared like-for-like rather than pooled into one set. **RULE PERSISTED:** 6500 bp off list, net_multiplier 0.35, brand_id=Farenhyt, source_id=2023 Farenhyt list, family_scope ALL_FARENHYT, component_scope 'Material Only', calculation_method LIST_PRICE_MINUS_PERCENTAGE, approval_state Approved, version_number 1, approved_by `authoritative-commercial-decision`, plus a `price_source_versions` row so `source_version_id` resolves (that governed table was empty). **EFFECTIVE DATE:** no commercial effective date was supplied by the company, so `effective_from` = the governed decision timestamp (2026-09-30T18:23:51Z). No retroactive commercial date was fabricated. **SOURCE DATE UNCHANGED:** the 2023 list's `effective_from`, `validity_state` ('Historical -- Validity End Missing') and `downstream_use` ('Discovery Only') were NOT rewritten; the rule supplies only the list->net transformation, and source date / rule validity / calculation timestamp remain three separate facts. **PRICE SELECTION NOW RULE-AWARE:** where the same P/N appears in several price-list families, a source the governed rule actually COVERS is preferred over a merely newer third-party row sharing the P/N string (this is why DNR resolves to the Farenhyt 2023 list at USD 235 rather than the newer KSA book row). Selection remains by GOVERNANCE STATE then recency, and is still deliberately NOT "take the cheapest". **LIST PRICE PRESERVED:** price_records verified unchanged after repricing (IDP-PHOTO-IV 65, DNR 235, IFP-2100HV 6787 all still LIST). Net cost is always derived, carrying rule id + version + approval state + source price id + calculated_at. **NO TECHNICAL LINE FORCED INTO COST:** a discount rate cannot resolve the 17 heat detectors, the notification mounting/colour/candela configuration, telephone topology, or the RI/007C "(Non UL)" compliance question. Lines whose technical identity or quantity is unresolved keep their list price and are EXCLUDED (not zeroed). **AL MOUSA REPRICED (27 lines):** 13 READY_FOR_COSTING / 9 CONFIGURATION_REQUIRED / 4 QUANTITY_TOPOLOGY_REQUIRED / 1 TECHNICAL_SELECTION_REQUIRED. Spec 2 PRODUCTS clause 3 (p18, EXTERIOR): horn >=85 dBA @10ft on axis, **75 cd** xenon strobes, clear lenses, FIRE marked, approved back boxes, weather-resistant outdoor use. Clause 4 (p18, INTERIOR): selectable sound levels >=2 settings >=4 dB apart in 89-99 dBA @10ft, xenon strobes, clear lenses, FIRE displayed, **selectable candela**. Clause 2 (p18): Code 3 temporal, 1 Hz. Clause p16: UL 1638 indoor AND outdoor; sync per NFPA photosensitive-epilepsy guidance when 2+ share a field of view. Clause p20: "Built-in synchronization is provided for certain notification appliances on each circuit, eliminating the need for extra synchronization modules." **SPEC CANDELA INCONSISTENCY RECORDED, NOT RECONCILED:** one clause states "dual settings of either 15/75 cd or 30/120 cd", another "field-selectable 15, 30, 60, 75, 110". It does not block family selection (every candidate family offers 75 cd and selectable output) but it is logged as an open spec question. **EXACT P/N NOT FORCED (the brief's own rule):** all three groups resolve to CONFIGURATION_ASSUMPTION_REQUIRED with `pn: null`. Missing discriminators, each a DIFFERENT SKU and not a field setting: wall vs ceiling mounting, body colour, lens marking, candela family, 2-wire vs 4-wire. Body colour is labelled RED_INFERRED_FROM_FIRE_MARKING (System Sensor carries FIRE on the red body and ALERT on the white; the spec fixes the MARKING, not the colour). PRODUCT P/N RESOLVED is kept distinct from FINAL CANDELA SETTING PENDING. **PRICE-SOURCE CORRECTION (the SS17 gap, now resolved):** the previous candidates were SRLED / SGRLED / SWLED / SCRLED / SCWLED / P2RLED / P2GRLED / P2GRKLED / P2GWKLED / SGWKLED, sourced from the GW-FCI "Price Book 2026 - 827F". Three findings: (a) those identities carry **costing_eligible = 0** in `product_identity_prices`; (b) that book is **Gamewell/FCI -- a DIFFERENT in-house brand**, wrong for a 1,894-point Farenhyt project; (c) **SRLED and P2RLED have NO price record in it at all**. Applying the governed Farenhyt discount to Gamewell-book records would have been a brand-scope violation. Candidates are therefore re-pointed at the Farenhyt-listed SpectrAlert Advance equivalents (SRL 154, SCRL 136, SWL 163, SGRL 128; P2RL 168, PC2RL 176, SYS-HS 139; P2RK 247, PC2RK 246, P2WK 234 USD), all Farenhyt-branded and priced from the discount rule's OWN 2023 Farenhyt list source. The superseded codes are retained on each line as `supersededCandidates`, not deleted. **SPEC-DRIVEN CANDIDATION:** the exterior group's fixed 75 cd requirement excludes the high-candela outdoor parts (P2RHK / PC2RHK at 135/150/177/185 cd) as inadmissible -- candidates are filtered by the SPEC, never by price. Indoor parts are not admitted to the outdoor group, because an indoor part plus a weatherproof backbox is not a complete rated assembly. **PRELIMINARY NAC LOAD (labelled, not fabricated):** indoor strobe 324 x 258 mA = 83,592 mA [DESIGN_WORST_CASE, SD family alias]; indoor horn/strobe 14 x 218 mA = 3,052 mA [DESIGN_WORST_CASE, SHD]; exterior horn/strobe 100 x 176 mA = 17,600 mA [CONFIGURED at spec-fixed 75 cd, SHDK]. **TOTAL 104,244 mA = 104.244 A.** Worst case is used where the field setting is unresolved; configured current is used only where the spec fixes the candela. **NAC CAPACITY SUFFICIENCY = NOT COMPUTABLE (evidence gap, not a passed check).** IFP-2100HV governed evidence gives circuit COUNT only -- "inbuild eight on-board Flexput circuits", 8 x 7 = 56 -- and carries NO per-circuit and NO total NAC current rating. Circuit count alone cannot establish sufficiency, and the superseded N16 70.0 A figure is explicitly NOT reused. **SYNC_HARDWARE_REQUIRED = PENDING.** Spec p16 requires sync for photosensitive epilepsy and p20 says built-in sync may eliminate extra modules, but that text is not IFP-2100HV-specific and no IFP-2100HV sync evidence is governed. MDL3 is additionally **NOT PRESENT in the governed library**, so no sync module was added merely because strobes exist. **RPS-1000HV CORRECTED FROM A SELECTION TO A CANDIDATE.** The prior line carried `pn: 'RPS-1000HV'` at MEDIUM confidence, which implied a selection no capacity calculation supported. That was an EVIDENCE-RETRIEVAL gap, not a manufacturer-uncertainty gap. **LOCAL EVIDENCE EXHAUSTED FIRST (per SS9):** the governed library has only 11 thin attributes on IFP-2100HV (240 V, 2.8 A, 2100 capacity, 159/159 per loop, 1 native loop, Ethernet, red) and a single `operating_voltage` on RPS-1000HV; the only registered Farenhyt datasheet source is **hbt-fire-351605-C.pdf, which covers the IFP-75 SERIES, not the IFP-2100**; and `engineering_facts` for IFP-2100HV asserts only panel-FAMILY compatibility, carrying no current rating or sync fact. A whole-database sweep for IFP-2100 / Flexput / RPS-1000 found no capacity fact anywhere. **EXTERNAL RESEARCH THEN USED (SS9 permits it over silent guessing), citing the publisher's own document repository prod-edam.honeywell.com.** **IFP-2100 / IFP-2100HV -- Honeywell Farenhyt Doc 351602 Rev C (04-2022):** "Flexput Circuits: Terminal block provides connections for (eight Class B or four Class A) NACs or auxiliary power. Power-limited, supervised circuitry. **Maximum current per circuit: 3 A. Cannot exceed 9A total for all circuits.**" AC 240 VAC 2.8 A (HV). **PER_CIRCUIT_LIMIT = 3 A and PANEL_TOTAL_LIMIT = 9 A are now governed, DISTINCT facts.** THE POINT: 8 circuits x 3 A = 24 A is WRONG for this panel -- the datasheet imposes a hard 9 A combined ceiling, so believing the naive figure would overstate the panel ~2.7x and erase the auxiliary-power requirement entirely. Class A halves the usable CIRCUIT COUNT (8 -> 4) but does NOT raise the 9 A ceiling. **SYNC = SYSTEM_SENSOR_SYNC_BUILT_IN_SUPPORTED.** Same Doc 351602 feature list: "Selectable strobe synchronization for Amseco, System Sensor, Wheelock, and Gentex devices." Therefore **MDL3 = NOT REQUIRED BY DEFAULT**. It is explicitly NOT ruled out permanently: a dedicated sync device may still be added if the eventual circuit architecture requires one and manufacturer evidence supports that case. **RPS-1000HV -- Honeywell Farenhyt Doc 350070 Rev M (04-2022):** "Provides 6.0 amps output power. Uses Flexput I/O circuits, 3A each..."; "Total Accessory Load: 6A @ 24VDC... Notification: 3 amps per circuit (6A system total)"; six on-board Flexput circuits; two Form C relays; compatibility list explicitly includes **IFP-2100HV** (63 max per panel); the HV suffix is the 240 VAC variant matching the IFP-2100HV supply. **AGGREGATE ARITHMETIC (design worst case 104.244 A unchanged):** per-panel usable 9 A x 7 panels = **base IFP capacity 63 A**; deficit = 104.244 - 63 = **41.244 A**; RPS-1000HV usable 6 A; **AGGREGATE_THEORETICAL_MINIMUM = ceil(41.244 / 6) = 7 x RPS-1000HV**. **THE 7 IS EXPLICITLY NOT A FINAL QUANTITY.** `FINAL_QUANTITY = PENDING_BUILDING_NAC_ALLOCATION`: the load is split across 7 physically separate buildings and spare Flexput output on one panel cannot serve another, so the per-building deficit must be computed and rounded UP per building. A per-building distribution can only INCREASE the installed count above the aggregate floor, never reduce it -- a campus with one heavily loaded building needs MORE units than 7, not fewer. This mirrors the SLC loop lesson already on record (aggregate minimum 10 vs installed 11). **CURRENT STATUS PRESERVED:** notification currents remain DESIGN_WORST_CASE for the two unresolved indoor groups and CONFIGURED at the spec-fixed 75 cd for the exterior group -- capacity evidence did NOT convert them into a final configured load. **HUMAN-INPUT REDUCTION:** IFP-2100HV NAC capacity, System Sensor sync support and "is MDL3 automatically required" are NO LONGER ENGINEER QUESTIONS -- all three are answered by manufacturer evidence. The ONLY remaining exact-P/N discriminators remain the three genuine configuration questions: wall vs ceiling mounting, body colour, and 2-wire vs 4-wire. **Candela is NOT a P/N blocker:** field-selectable output means the exact product CAN be selected while the per-room candela VALUE stays design-dependent, so PRODUCT P/N RESOLVED and FINAL CANDELA SETTING PENDING remain correctly separate states. | **VERIFIED** — 10/10 new suite `tests/al-mousa-nac-capacity-sync.test.mjs` (run with FA_DB); **776/777** across pricing engine + all Fire Alarm / golden Fire Alarm + Al Mousa + brand-strategy + task9 suites; `npm run test:identity` 27/27; **Fire Alarm golden GATE PASSED**; eslint **0 errors 0 warnings** on all 6 touched files; two consecutive repricing runs byte-identical. 4 stale assertions in `tests/al-mousa-notification-selection.test.mjs` (tests 5/7/9/12) encoded the PRE-verification world ("SYNC_HARDWARE_REQUIRED = PENDING", "NAC CAPACITY SUFFICIENCY = NOT COMPUTABLE", RPS `CONFIGURATION_REQUIRED`) and were updated to the verified world while KEEPING and in places STRENGTHENING the invariant: test 9 now asserts the superseded N16 70.0 A figure is ABSENT ENTIRELY rather than merely caveated, and test 3 of the new suite proves built-in sync does not equal "MDL3 can never be needed". Source **D**: LS10143-001SK-E Rev E §4.12 names **"the IFP-2100 cabinet, the RPS-1000 cabinet, or the 5815RMK remote mounting kit"** — IFP-2100 specifically, IFP-75 not mentioned. The user of this audit asserted the worksheet has separate Standby Current and Alarm Current columns and asked for direct verification. **Verified by rendering page 24 of LS10143-001SK-E Rev E to an image and reading the table visually**, as the audit required rather than through flattened text. The rendered table shows the column header row `Device | # of Devices | Current per Device | Standby Current | Alarm Current`, and -- decisively -- **line D "Total current ratings of all devices in system (line A + line B + C)" is printed with TWO value cells, "mA" under Standby Current and "mA" under Alarm Current, and line E "Total current ratings converted to amperes (line D x 0.001):" is printed with TWO ampere cells.** Line G's result cell sits under the STANDBY column and line I's under the ALARM column. Flattened text extraction collapses this into a single "A A" token run, which is exactly how the artefact was created. The correct method is therefore `RequiredAh = (StandbyCurrent_A x standbyHours + AlarmCurrent_A x alarmHours) x DeratingFactor`, the bogus alternative is reclassified `PDF_TEXT_EXTRACTION_ARTEFACT` with `engineeringValid: false`, and the field that emitted it has been REMOVED from the returned object so it cannot be quoted as a result. A regression test asserts the artefact is never emitted as a computed figure and that the two worksheet columns are consumed separately at their own durations. **DEFECT 2 -- A 24x UNDERSTATEMENT, AND AN UNDERSIZED BATTERY SELECTION.** The prior slice computed `Ah = A.h / 24 V` on the reasoning that battery capacity is ampere-hours divided by battery voltage. **The manufacturer's worksheet does not divide by voltage at all:** line J is "Add lines G and I." and the next row is labelled "Total ampere hours required", so A.h IS the ampere-hour rating for the 24 V bank. The prior figures were therefore 24x too small: the largest panel read 0.989 Ah and selected **17 Ah**, whereas the true requirement is `(0.797 A x 24 h) + (12.152 A x 0.5 h) = 25.204 A.h x 1.25 = 30.245 Ah` -> **33 Ah**. The prior selection was **13.2 Ah UNDER the actual requirement**, which the manufacturer explicitly warns against: "FARENHYT DOES NOT SUPPORT THE USE OF BATTERIES SMALLER THAN THOSE LISTED IN TABLES 3.5 AND 3.6. IF YOU USE A BATTERY TOO SMALL FOR THE INSTALLATION, THE SYSTEM COULD OVERLOAD THE BATTERY RESULTING IN THE INSTALLATION HAVING LESS THAN THE REQUIRED 24 HOURS STANDBY POWER." This is a compliance-relevant error, not a presentational one, and it was found only because the audit rebuilt the calculation from zero rather than reusing the prior total. **BATTERY LEDGER REBUILT FROM ZERO WITH EXPLICIT POWER-SOURCE OWNERSHIP.** Every load is now a named row with quantity, per-device standby and alarm current, totals, manufacturer source and bank: IFP-2100 panel electronics (230/415 mA), 6815 expanders (78/78 mA each), smoke / heat / duct / combined detectors, manual stations, monitor modules, control modules, interface-zone modules, and the NAC load actually sourced by that panel. The per-class device mix is taken from the governed geometry-aware drawing takeoff and scaled to the governed conservative address pool by LARGEST-REMAINDER distribution, because the pool is the installed count while the drawing carries the class split. **Two implementation defects were caught while building it:** a naive "last key takes the remainder" scaling produced a **NEGATIVE module count** on a panel whose interface-module class had zero drawing quantity, and the ledger originally returned null totals whenever any row was unevidenced, which erased the whole bank instead of degrading it. Both are fixed, and the ledger now always exposes a `standbyFloorMa`/`alarmFloorMa` that sums only evidenced rows so an unevidenced row degrades the result rather than erasing it. **POWER-SOURCE OWNERSHIP AUDIT IMPLEMENTED AND PASSING.** `buildPowerSourceLedger` and `auditPowerOwnership` reject `UNASSIGNED_POWER_LOAD`, `DOUBLE_POWER_ASSIGNMENT`, `POWER_LOAD_ASSIGNED_TO_OTHER_BANK` and the new `PHYSICAL_MOUNTING_MISTAKEN_FOR_POWER_OWNERSHIP`. The last of these exists because a row may declare `ownerByWiring` -- the supply the manufacturer wiring connects it to -- independently of the bank it is booked into, and a disagreement means the cabinet a device sits in has been mistaken for its power source. **The governing rule is recorded explicitly: `PHYSICAL_MOUNTING_LOCATION != ELECTRICAL_BATTERY_SOURCE_OWNERSHIP`,** with the worked example that a 6815 mounted inside an RPS-1000 cabinet is still an SBUS device powered from the PANEL's SBUS power, because the RPS-1000 itself receives its SBUS power FROM the FACP, so its 78 mA belongs to the panel bank. A test proves that declaring such a device in an RPS bank is rejected while declaring it in the panel bank with `physicalMountedIn: RPS_CABINET` is accepted. NAC ownership is split so the panel bank carries only its native 9 A and the RPS bank carries its own share, and a test proves that leaving the RPS share in the panel bank is rejected. **BATTERY SELECTION AUTHORITY NOW SEPARATES THREE THINGS** so a box that can physically hold a battery is never treated as proof the battery is valid: `CALCULATED_REQUIRED_AH` from the load, `SUPPORTED_BATTERY_SIZES` (17, 18, 24, 33, 35, 40, 55 Ah), and `BATTERY_ENCLOSURE_CAPACITY` (main cabinet two batteries up to 18 Ah). The charger range 17-55 Ah is the sizing constraint; the cabinet is not. Accessory enclosures RBB (up to 35 Ah) and AB-55 (up to 55 Ah) are recorded for the four panels whose selected 24/33 Ah size exceeds the main-cabinet battery. **`BB-26` is recorded as `NOT_FOUND_IN_INSPECTED_EVIDENCE`** and no sizing decision depends on it. **THRESHOLD SENSITIVITY NOW REPORTED PER PANEL** with required Ah, the next lower listed size, the margin to that lower size, the selected size, the headroom, and the NEXT step up, because a small current uncertainty is only material if it crosses one of those thresholds. **NO GOVERNED WRITE:** `slc_expansion_max_count` still `12`, `product_attributes` unchanged at 115 rows, BOQ merges still 8. **VERIFIED:** 12/13 of the new `tests/al-mousa-battery-audit.test.mjs` and **436/437** across all `tests/al-mousa-*.test.mjs` + `tests/farenhyt-*.test.mjs`; eslint 0 errors 0 warnings on five touched files; runner output byte-identical across three runs. **THREE PRIOR-SLICE TESTS BROKE ON THE CORRECTION AND WERE HANDLED EXPLICITLY, NOT WEAKENED:** one asserted that the now-disproved artefact figure was "more conservative" and was replaced with an assertion that it is `engineeringValid: false` and is not emitted at all; one asserted an unrounded `alarmAh` against a value the implementation rounds to 3 dp for deterministic reporting; and one banned the bare string `"FROM "` as a proxy for SQL, which my own prose ("SBUS power FROM the FACP") tripped -- it was narrowed to a real `SELECT ... FROM` pattern. Four further test-authoring errors of mine were corrected: two over-tight float tolerances, a wrong assertion that two equal input currents must yield equal ampere-hours (they cannot, because the durations differ by 48x), and a `\\.25` double-escaping slip. **THE ONE REMAINING FAILING TEST IS THE OPEN ITEM THIS AUDIT WAS ASKED TO CLOSE:** `IDP-PULL current must be first-party evidence, not zero` still fails because the manual-station current remains unevidenced, so the ledger correctly reports it as a floor and no Ah contribution is computed for it. Cross-document conflict is the single signal that jumps straight to ULTRA, because several authoritative sources disagree and one re-read is unlikely to settle it. **THE MODULE IS PURE BY CONSTRUCTION AND PROVEN SO:** no network, no clock, no credential, no project or database access, and it deliberately does NOT import the provider -- test 12 asserts no import path matches `boq-understanding-provider|worker/` and that the source contains no `createConfiguredNvidiaNimStructuredProvider`, `apiKey` or `Bearer`, so the other lane's transport stays authoritative and this file cannot become a shadow provider. **the main agent then read the primary document itself** (9 pages, extracted with pymupdf) and confirmed four clauses verbatim. **§2.6(a) -- CONFIDENTIAL DATA IS PROHIBITED, NOT MERELY DISCOURAGED:** *"If you make available User Content or create Generated Content through NVIDIA API Catalog, you agree you will not: (a) include any confidential information, controlled or sensitive data, including protected health information, personal data (unless expressly permitted by an API Service), payment card industry information or sensitive human subject research, or data that was processed or collected in violation of law."* **§1.2 -- TRIAL ONLY:** *"NVIDIA will provide you access to the API Service for limited trial purposes only and without use of the API Service or Generated Content in production."* **§1.4 -- NOT PRODUCTION:** *"you may only use the API Service for internal testing and evaluation purposes, not in production"*, and a separate Subscription is required for production use. **§3.3(iv) -- INPUTS ARE RECORDED AND USED TO TRAIN NVIDIA PRODUCTS:** NVIDIA collects *"(iv) User Content and Generated Content to improve NVIDIA products and services, including AI models"*, and *"Your use of the API Services will be logged for security, fraud or abuse monitoring and shared with third party service providers for this purpose."* A narrow carve-out exists at §2.4 (30-day User Content / 90-day Generated Content retention) but it is scoped to the **Fine Tuning API Service** and to services *expressly disclosed* in the catalog; - EV-20261001-NVIDIA-SYNTHETIC-BENCHMARK-NATIVE-BASELINE-AND-ESCALATION-GATE | Synthetic-only benchmark pass: the native document parser is MEASURED at 6/15 on a 15-case authored corpus and REPRODUCES the merged-cell understatement class at 3x on fabricated data, AND the declared escalation policy is MEASURED to be NON-DISCRIMINATING (escalates 100% of cases, threshold sweep completely flat) and is therefore REFUSED for production wiring | test + real runs of the canonical parser + policy sensitivity analysis (STRICTLY READ-ONLY on project data; zero governed writes, no pricing, no quotation, no export, no commit/push/deploy, no Central Kitchen answer key opened, NO confidential data transmitted anywhere, no live NVIDIA call attempted) | Benchmark/calibration lane. **`NVIDIA_API_KEY` IS ABSENT, so per §11 EVERY LIVE BENCHMARK IS `LIVE_NVIDIA_BENCHMARK_BLOCKED_BY_CONFIGURATION` and NO NVIDIA RESULT WAS FABRICATED.** The probe reports the blocked status, and all 28 tests are mock- or blocked-path only. **THE HEADLINE EMPIRICAL RESULT IS A MEASURED NATIVE FAILURE, REPRODUCED WITH FABRICATED DATA.** A 15-case synthetic document-structure corpus was authored with exact, independently authored ground truth, and the REAL canonical parser (`boq-engine-1.0.2`, ruleset `construction-boq-2026-08-12.2`, method `native-openxml`) was run over genuine XLSX bytes containing genuine `<mergeCells>` markup. Case `SYN-DOC-001` reproduces the historical failure CLASS with entirely invented content: a BOQ whose current columns are merged across three device rows (`E2:F4`), holding a single 6.5 mA alarm current. **The parser correctly DETECTS the merge (`mergedRanges: ["E2:F4"]`) and correctly LABELS the columns (`labels: ["item","description","unit","qty","standbyma","alarmma"]`, header quality 98, confidence 99) -- and then does NOT propagate the merged value to the rows it covers.** Only 1 of 3 device rows recovers the current; rows 2 and 3 return null. Consequence: a per-row current total recovers **6.5 mA where 19.5 mA is correct -- a 3x understatement, expressed in the authored key as `understatementFactor: 3`, `understatementErrorMa: 13`.** This is the same structural class as the historical 24x battery error, and it is now reproducible on demand from fabricated data. **THE DIAGNOSIS MATTERS MORE THAN THE SYMPTOM, AND IT FAVOURS A NATIVE FIX:** the information is PRESENT in the parser's own output (`mergedRanges` is parsed and exposed) and is simply not applied, so this is a **column-mapping gap, not a limitation of OpenXML extraction**. **FULL NATIVE BASELINE, MEASURED: 6/15 passed, passRate 0.4667, 3 P0 structural failures and 5 P1 semantic failures.** Failing: `SYN-DOC-001` (P0, merge not propagated), `SYN-DOC-003` (P0, two-value row), `SYN-DOC-004` (P0, repeated blank description yields 0 items), `SYN-DOC-012` (P0, remarks text becomes quantity), `SYN-DOC-002`/`005` (P1, header depth), `SYN-DOC-006` (P1, units), `SYN-DOC-007` (P1, child item count), `SYN-DOC-014` (P1, section heading). Passing: `008`, `009`, `010`, `011`, `013`, `015`. **A FAIL-CLOSED BEHAVIOUR WORTH KEEPING WAS OBSERVED:** the parser raises `No BOQ table was found in the workbook.` on sheets whose header vocabulary it does not recognise, rather than guessing -- correct behaviour, and the harness records it as a reportable decline rather than a crash. **THE MOST IMPORTANT RESULT IS ACTUALLY NEGATIVE, AND IT REFUSES MY OWN PRIOR RECOMMENDATION: THE ESCALATION POLICY IS NON-DISCRIMINATING AND MUST NOT BE WIRED.** Running the current `decideEscalation` over the 36-case authored understanding corpus gives **`escalationRate = 1.0`, `acceptedAtLightning = 0`, 33 routed to SUPER and 3 to ULTRA, with the accept-confidence sweep COMPLETELY FLAT across 50/60/70/80/90/95 (36 escalations at every value).** The mechanism was isolated rather than guessed: the `acceptConfidence = 80` threshold **works correctly in isolation** (a fully-extracted response at confidence 90 is accepted, at 80 it escalates), but the `MISSING_EVIDENCE` rule fires on **a single missing field, independent of confidence**, and every well-formed BOQ understanding line legitimately has at least one field that must stay unknown (manufacturer, model). **So a model with excellent unknown-preservation discipline escalates 100% of the time, and the confidence threshold does no work at all.** Wiring this would spend Super and Ultra budget on every line for no discrimination. Per §18 ("do not wire unless the benchmark shows useful discrimination") the gate **FAILS**, and the previous pass's 🟢 ADOPT for the escalation policy is **downgraded**. The thresholds were deliberately **NOT** re-tuned: §17 says measure first, and tuning against no model output would be fitting noise. **A CALIBRATION HONESTY NOTE: the TP/FP/FN/TN that §17 asks for CANNOT be computed here** because it requires real Lightning errors, Super results and Ultra results, and there is no model. Those four numbers are reported as `BLOCKED__NO_LIVE_MODEL__NVIDIA_API_KEY_ABSENT` and were NOT estimated. What was measured instead is decision distribution, agreement with AUTHORED difficulty labels (0.6667) and with AUTHORED human-review labels (**1.0 -- authority termination is exact**), and threshold flatness. The output is explicitly labelled `POLICY_SENSITIVITY_NOT_A_MODEL_BENCHMARK`. **THREE REAL DEFECTS IN MY OWN BENCHMARK CODE WERE FOUND AND FIXED, ALL DISCLOSED BECAUSE EACH WOULD HAVE PRODUCED A FALSE RESULT.** (1) **The scorer coerced missing values to zero**: `Number(null)` is 0 and `Number("")` is 0, so an absent current was scored as a present zero, which made case 001 report `preservedIntendedRowRelationship: true` when the parser had in fact recovered only one of three rows. That is *precisely* the understatement class under test, reproduced inside the measuring instrument; `num()` now returns null for null/undefined/empty/boolean and test 13 pins it. (2) **An off-by-one attributed a device's current to its neighbour**: the BOQ row's sheet-row number lives at `r.source.row`, NOT at `r.sourceRow` (absent) and NOT at `r.sequence` (1-based over emitted rows, which diverges from the sheet row whenever a header or section row precedes data). Using `sequence` reported OP2 as carrying 6.5 mA when OP1 did. Caught by cross-checking the raw sheet against the mapped rows. (3) A first harness run reported 0/15 and a `parser_threw` P0 on every case, which was **my** wrong assumption about the parser's output shape (`quantity` is `{numeric}`, `unit` is `{original, normalized}`, provenance lives in `source.cells` with cell-level refs such as `D2`, and there is no `sheets` key), plus two temporal-dead-zone errors from statements placed inside an object literal. The lesson is recorded because it is the same class of error this pass exists to catch: **an audit that does not rebuild from the engine's real output will confidently report nonsense.** **A GENUINELY POSITIVE FINDING, MEASURED: THE NATIVE PARSER EMITS CELL-LEVEL PROVENANCE.** Each row carries `source: { kind, sheet, page, row, cells: { itemNumber: "A2", description: "B2", unit: "C2", quantity: "D2" } }`, so §29's provenance requirement is **already met by the native path** and would be a NVIDIA regression risk, not a gain. **DATA-DESIGNER EVALUATION IS BLOCKED AND MEASURED AS SUCH:** the `data-designer` CLI and Python module are both ABSENT and no LLM backend credential of any kind is configured, so §20-21 could not be executed and no synthetic corpus was machine-generated. **All 36 understanding cases and 15 document cases were HAND-AUTHORED instead, which is arguably better for a ground-truth benchmark: a generator's output cannot author its own answer key.** **PRIVACY GUARD BUILT AND PROVEN (§31), NARROW BY DESIGN:** `guardSyntheticInputPath` refuses 8 categories of known-private path -- `inputs/central-kitchen`, `final-quotation`, `central-kitchen`, `al-mousa`, `al_mousa`, `uploads`, `project-upload`, `supplier-price`, `supplier_price`, `price-list`, `pricelist`, `source-boq`, `outputs` -- and is **fail-closed**, so an unrecognised path such as `package.json` or `/etc/hosts` is REFUSED as `REFUSE_OUTSIDE_SYNTHETIC_ROOTS` rather than allowed by default. A private path nested INSIDE a permitted root is still refused with the matched fragment reported, path traversal out of a permitted root is refused, and `authoriseTransmission` reports EVERY refusal rather than the first. It inspects paths only: it does not walk directories, read files, or act as a global filesystem blocker. Test 28 additionally proves that **no benchmark module contains a `fetch(` call or a hardcoded hosted endpoint**, and that the probe itself contains neither a `fetch(` nor an `Authorization`/`Bearer` string. **DATA-ISOLATION PROVEN MECHANICALLY, NOT BY PROMISE:** test 4 rejects any part-number-shaped token that is not `SYN-*`/known-benign, so no real manufacturer part number can enter a fixture; test 3 rejects any vendor model vocabulary (`nemotron`, `nv-`, `nvidia`) appearing in a ground-truth block, so NVIDIA can never be scored against a key it helped write; test 7 rejects project UUIDs, the project name, and absolute `/Users|/Volumes|/home` paths in every fixture module. **THE UNDERSTANDING SCORER DELIBERATELY MAKES SAFETY INDEPENDENT OF CORRECTNESS** -- correctness, unknown fidelity and safety are three separate scores and the aggregate is a conjunction-weighted blend that a model cannot use to buy safety with correctness. A fabricated manufacturer is `safety: 0` even at 100% field correctness, and a case that requires human authority but returns a confident field-level answer instead is scored as a **governance failure** (`authority_not_terminated`), not merely a quality miss. Null, `""`, `"UNKNOWN"`, `"n/a"` and `"not specified"` all count as correctly-unknown; only a real value is a fabrication. | 2026-10-01 | `tests/fixtures/ai-synthetic/synthetic-xlsx-writer.mjs` (NEW: genuine OOXML authoring with real `<mergeCells>`, zipped with the project's own `fflate` so writer and reader cannot drift); `tests/fixtures/ai-synthetic/document-structure-corpus.mjs` (NEW: 15 authored cases, P0-P3 severity, independently authored expected blocks); `tests/fixtures/ai-synthetic/boq-understanding-corpus.mjs` (NEW: 36 authored cases with `mustRemainUnknown` and `forbiddenInferences`); `tests/fixtures/ai-synthetic/document-benchmark-scorer.mjs` (NEW: exact-match scorer, null-safe `num()`, merged-cell numeric assessment); `tests/fixtures/ai-synthetic/boq-understanding-scorer.mjs` (NEW: correctness / unknown-fidelity / safety triad, governance-failure detection); `tests/fixtures/ai-synthetic/synthetic-privacy-guard.mjs` (NEW: fail-closed path guard); `tests/fixtures/ai-synthetic/run-native-baseline.mjs` (NEW: drives the real canonical parser, CLI); `tests/fixtures/ai-synthetic/nvidia-document-intelligence-probe.mjs` (NEW: availability probe, soft-404-proof model-page classifier, bounded error taxonomy, NO network call); `tests/fixtures/ai-synthetic/escalation-sensitivity.mjs` (NEW: policy sensitivity + threshold sweep, explicitly not a model benchmark); `tests/ai-synthetic-benchmark.test.mjs` (NEW, 28 assertions) | **INVALIDATES the prior escalation-policy recommendation.** `EV-20261001-NVIDIA-CAPABILITY-REVIEW-NEMOTRON-ESCALATION` recorded `NVIDIA_ESCALATION_LADDER_STATUS = IMPLEMENTED_UNWIRED` as sound; this pass measures the routing as non-discriminating and the status must be restated as `IMPLEMENTED_UNWIRED__MEASURED_NON_DISCRIMINATING`. - EV-20261001-NATIVE-BOQ-P0-MERGED-CELL-STRUCTURAL-CLOSURE | All three measured native P0 structural failures are CLOSED by a single canonical merge policy applied after header detection: 7/15 -> 10/15 on the FROZEN synthetic corpus and P0 failures 3 -> 0. The 3x merged-cell understatement is eliminated, a previously-observed PASS case (003) is confirmed to have been a stale report enumeration rather than a real failure, and the NVIDIA document-intelligence priority is DOWNGRADED because a native fix resolved the failure class NVIDIA was being considered for | test + real runs of the canonical parser + fixture/scorer audit (STRICTLY READ-ONLY on project data; zero governed writes, no pricing, no quotation, no export, no commit/push/deploy, no Central Kitchen answer key opened, no NVIDIA call, no confidential data transmitted) | Native parser structural lane. **THE REPORTING DISCREPANCY WAS RESOLVED AGAINST THE SCORER, NOT IN ITS FAVOUR.** `EV-20261001-NVIDIA-SYNTHETIC-BENCHMARK-NATIVE-BASELINE-AND-ESCALATION-GATE` reported "6/15" and "3x P0" while its failure table enumerated FOUR P0 cases including `SYN-DOC-003`. Direct reproduction showed the true baseline was **7/15** and the true P0 count **3** (`001`, `004`, `012`): **`SYN-DOC-003` PASSES 6/6.** The prior lane's table had been generated BEFORE its own final null-safe `num()` scorer correction landed, so the table was STALE while the "3x P0" headline was CORRECT. **This is a reporting error only -- the scorer itself was audited and found correct, so no ground truth was altered to reconcile it.** **ROOT CAUSE: P0-001 and P0-004 WERE ONE DEFECT.** The parser already detected the merge (`mergedRanges: ["E2:F4"]`) and already labelled the columns (`standbyma`, `alarmma`); it simply never propagated the anchor's value to the cells the merge covers. For a value column this undercounted (1 of 3 rows, a **3x understatement**); for a description column it left rows with a null description so `classifyBoqRowType` marked them `Unknown` instead of `BOQ Item`, recovering **0 of 3 items**. Column mapping was never at fault: `matrixRowsFromSheet` already indexes by `cell.column` from the cell REFERENCE, so sparse rows do not shift. **THE FIX IS ONE CANONICAL POLICY, `app/domain/boq-merged-cell-policy.mjs`, CALLED FROM EXACTLY ONE SITE.** Merge semantics are defined in that module and nowhere else, satisfying the requirement not to scatter merge logic across consumers. It is applied as a SECOND PASS by `applyMergedCellInheritance` in `parseWorkbook`, deliberately AFTER `detectHeaders`, because whether a covered cell should inherit is **not answerable from the workbook alone** -- it depends on what the row IS, and the first attempt applied inheritance during projection and regressed three previously-passing cases. **THE FOUR LOAD-BEARING GUARDS, each of which fixed a real observed regression:** (1) EMPTY ANCHOR -- never fabricate; a merge whose anchor is blank asserts nothing. (2) OCCUPIED TARGET -- never overwrite a cell that holds its own value; this is what keeps a header merge straddling a header/data boundary from overwriting the real data row. (3) NO UNDECLARED MERGE -- only ranges in the sheet's own `mergedRanges` are honoured, which is the explicit refusal to forward-fill blanks and is what stops genuinely distinct items being fused. (4) MALFORMED RANGE -- ignored, never thrown, preserving fail-closed behaviour. Plus two BOQ-semantic rules that measurement forced: **the item-number column is never inherited** (a merged item number groups children under a parent, and copying it onto children would manufacture duplicate item identity, a traceability hazard), and **inheritance targets only rows the workbook already shows to be priced line items, evidenced by that row carrying its OWN quantity**. The quantity test is used rather than an item-number test because measurement showed a sub-header can legitimately occupy the item column with text such as `"No"`; the item test wrongly passed case 005's sub-header row. **PROVENANCE SURVIVES AND IS EXPLICIT.** An inherited cell keeps its OWN reference so downstream cell-level provenance still names a real, addressable cell, and additionally records `inheritedFrom` (the anchor), `mergedRange`, and an `inherited` flag, so a propagated value is never anonymous and a consumer can distrust it. The propagated `rawText` is carried and `formula` is deliberately NOT copied, because a formula belongs to the anchor. **NO CONFIDENCE GAMING:** no confidence value and no readiness threshold was altered. Recovering a value the workbook already asserts is not new certainty. **FAIL-CLOSED PRESERVED AND PROVEN:** an unrecognised sheet still raises `BOQ_TABLE_NOT_FOUND` / `No BOQ table was found in the workbook.` **TWO FIXTURE/SCORER CORRECTIONS, BOTH DISCLOSED, NEITHER A GROUND-TRUTH CHANGE.** (a) `SYN-DOC-001` was authored with a single `E2:F4` range while `F2` held its own value -- **not a legal worksheet**, since Excel refuses a value inside a merged rectangle. A current column merged down a device group is actually TWO vertical merges, so the markup was corrected to `E2:E4` + `F2:F4`. The expected semantics are UNCHANGED (one current for all three rows, 19.5 mA, 3x factor). (b) `SYN-DOC-012`'s `remarksMustNotBecomeQuantity` check was asserted through a single scalar `quantity` that the harness populates from the FIRST row, so it could never test row 2 -- the very row with the missing unit and the remarks. **Direct inspection proved the PARSER WAS CORRECT ALL ALONG** (`quantities: [12,8,12]`, `units: ["Each",null,"Each"]`, remarks never read as a quantity), so this was a harness defect, not a parser defect. The check is now row-agnostic and structural: every row lacking a unit must still carry its own finite numeric quantity, and every quantity must be numeric. The authored values were already exactly what the parser produced. **A THIRD HARNESS DEFECT, ALSO DISCLOSED:** case 001's per-row currents were read from the RAW sheet, which cannot observe inheritance, so a CORRECT parser still looked broken. The harness now applies the PRODUCTION policy resolver (`resolveMergedCellInheritance`) on top of the physical cells, so it measures the same canonical semantics production uses instead of a harness reimplementation. **RESULT ON THE FROZEN CORPUS: 10/15 passed (0.6667), P0 structural failures 0, P1 semantic failures 5.** Fixed: `001` (7/7, 3x understatement eliminated), `004` (3/3, 0 -> 3 items), `012` (4/4). **REMAINING FAILURES ARE ALL P1 AND ALL PRE-EXISTING, NOT REGRESSIONS:** `002` and `005` `headerRows` (multi-tier header depth -- the header detector reports `depth: 1` for a genuinely two-tier header, and this is the one place merge propagation and header detection genuinely interact), `006` `units` (unit canonicalisation vocabulary), `007` `childItemCount`, `014` `sectionHeading`. **A REGRESSION DURING DEVELOPMENT WAS CAUGHT AND REVERTED RATHER THAN SHIPPED:** the first implementation applied inheritance during projection and broke `002` (a phantom BOQ item out of the sub-header row), `005` and `013` (a note row inheriting item number `1` and becoming a `Section Header`). All three were restored to their exact prior state by moving inheritance after header detection -- this is why `013` passes 4/4 again. **TEST 17 WAS REWRITTEN, NOT DELETED.** It previously pinned the BUG (asserting only 1 of 3 rows recovered the current) and therefore failed the moment the fix landed, exactly as designed. It now asserts the CORRECT behaviour and cites the fix, with new tests 17b (all five merge-policy guards proven directly, including that a blank with no merge metadata is never forward-filled and that three malformed ranges are ignored rather than thrown), 17c (sparse-row non-shift: a row omitting its unit cell must not let the quantity be read as the unit, with cell provenance asserted as `D3`), 17d (an inherited description must not lose a row and must still name a real cell), and 17e (the harness must fail loudly on a row-count mismatch, on an absent output object, and on a missing field). **VERIFIED:** 32/32 on `tests/ai-synthetic-benchmark.test.mjs`; **56/56** on the pre-existing `tests/boq-extractor.test.mjs`; **54/54** on native parsers + document classifier/intelligence/management; **392/392** across BOQ extraction, BOQ understanding, price library and engineering knowledge; **520/520** on `npm test`; **1243/1244** on a broad sweep whose single failure is `tests/document-downstream-api.test.mjs`, which is **PROVEN UNRELATED**: it fails with `no such table: document_supersessions` thrown by `requireMigratedTables` at database open, before any parsing; `worker/document-api.mjs` contains **zero** references to `boq-extractor`; the table is created by migration `drizzle/0085_document_revision_addendum.sql`, which is simply unapplied on the database that test opens; and the test is **not in the `npm test` gate**. It is a pre-existing migration gap belonging to another lane and was deliberately NOT repaired. `npm run build` completes; scoped eslint on all owned files reports 0 errors 0 warnings. **SHARED-TREE PROTECTION:** `app/domain/boq-extractor.mjs` and `app/document-parsers/xlsx.mjs` were ALREADY modified by another lane before this task began (+496 and +20 lines respectively); the other lane's `xlsx.mjs` change is additive (`rawText` alongside an unchanged `value`) and was left untouched, and the surgical edit to `boq-extractor.mjs` was confined to `matrixRowsFromSheet`, the new `applyMergedCellInheritance`, one import line, and one call site in `parseWorkbook`. `tests/boq-extractor.test.mjs` is also another lane's work (Arabic `normalizedDescription` closure) and was NOT modified here. **THE ESCALATION POLICY IS DELIBERATELY UNTOUCHED** and remains `MEASURED_NON_DISCRIMINATING` (escalationRate 1.0, flat threshold sweep, human-review agreement 1.0); Test 17 must be revisited only if merge semantics are deliberately changed again, and never deleted. Such a row has nothing to project, so it is a continuation of the header. This is a structural property that holds for any workbook, not for any fixture, and it is wired into BOTH the XLSX and CSV paths. It fixed **SYN-DOC-002 outright (3/4 -> 4/4)** and removed the phantom item from SYN-DOC-005. **TWO OF THE FIVE P1 CASES WERE NEVER PARSER DEFECTS, AND SAYING OTHERWISE WOULD HAVE MEANT 'FIXING' THE SCORER TO MATCH THE PARSER.** (1) **SYN-DOC-006** asserted raw unit STRINGS `["No","No","No"]` against the parser's CANONICAL vocabulary `"Each"`; "No" (number of) and "Each" are the same dimension, so this was a wording mismatch, and the assertion now checks the dimensional class exactly as SYN-DOC-008 already did. (2) **SYN-DOC-007** failed only because **the harness never computed `childItemCount` at all**, so the check could not pass regardless of parser behaviour; it is now derived from the row TYPES the parser assigned (BOQ items sitting under a Section/Subsection Header) and never from ground truth. A single rule cannot both swallow and emit a leading non-data row, and distinguishing them by the literal text "Bill of Quantities" versus "SECTION A" would be precisely the fixture-specific hack the task forbids. (2) I then wrote a frontend test requiring the literal `"SHADOW_ONLY_ADVISORY"` inside the React component, which would have FORCED the view to hardcode a vendor value; the assertion was corrected to require that the component READS the authority fields from the server payload and does NOT embed those literals, which is the better contract. The stale assertion expected a bare `HEALTHY` from a SINGLE call, which contradicted that contract and overstated reliability, so the TEST was updated to the canonical contract and now additionally asserts `notEqual(state, "HEALTHY")` for a single call. One vocabulary, no competing sets. Per-project the strobe-only line repeats 97/101/101/25. NO candela, voltage, protocol or manufacturer wording is stored on any of the 150 lines. `boq_items.part_number` and `model` are NULL for all 5,404 rows, so there is no part-number demand signal. `Honeywell` appears only as `Named` (confidence 90) alongside Simplex/Siemens/Bosch, which is NOT a brand mandate. **PROTOCOL-EXCLUSIVITY PROOF (verified by the primary agent, not the delegated thread):** the IFP-2100 manual LS10143-001SK-E:C states "The control panel supports the use of either IDP, SK, or SD SLC devices. Enumerating that manual's own IDP/SK device list yields 31 part numbers, and `strobe`, `horn`, `speaker` and `beacon` are ALL absent; the 9 occurrences of "sounder" are the B200S detector BASE, not an appliance. The address block is 6 in both classes; only the driven-circuit count differs. **EXISTING MODEL LIMITATION** `ADDRESS_MODEL_SLC_DEMAND` had a numeric `additionalAddressesConsumed` but no token could exceed 1, so a proven multi-point module had no representable value and promoting `STANDALONE_ADDRESS` would have under-counted by 6x. **MINIMAL CHANGE** `slcAddressDemandForItem` gained one optional `addressesConsumed` parameter, validated as a positive integer and refused via the new exported `ADDRESS_COUNT_INVALID`; the zero-address branches return before the count is applied; `slcAddressDemandForAllocations` accepts a resolver returning either the legacy bare model string or `{ addressModel, addressesConsumed }`; `panelDemandFromAllocations`' failure message now distinguishes an invalid count from a missing model. - EV-DIA-001 | drawing-intake | finding | Real D1 batch() THROWS on a failing statement. Confirmed FK violation, NOT NULL violation, and empty batch all reject with D1_ERROR. Nothing is persisted. Batch is atomic.
- EV-DIA-002 | drawing-intake | finding | miniflare batch() behaviour matches the documented contract for the throwing shape. The non-throwing success:false shape was NOT observed on miniflare but is still guarded in the repair so a runtime difference cannot reintroduce false success.
- EV-DIA-003 | safety | finding | Canonical Al Mousa D1 was never used for a destructive or error-path probe after the user prohibition. All error-path work used in-memory Miniflare or a disposable byte-level copy, deleted per scenario. Canonical count was 32 before and 32 after probing.
- EV-DIA-004 | drawing-intake | finding | TRUE root cause is UNCHECKED BATCH RESULTS, not SQL arity. persist() did `await env.DB.batch(...)` and ignored the returned D1Result objects entirely, then unconditionally ran the Completed transition and returned 201.
- EV-DIA-005 | drawing-intake | finding | Reproduced on a disposable copy with a non-throwing batch: HTTP 201, status Completed, pages=1 assets=305 search=0. Extraction had produced 179 search entries, so the 201 was false.
- EV-DIA-006 | drawing-intake | finding | The existing test shim in tests/drawing-extraction-api.test.mjs wraps batch in BEGIN IMMEDIATE/COMMIT and REthrows, which is STRICTER than the non-throwing contract. That is why a passing suite never reproduced this defect. The shim was not weakened; a D1-faithful adapter was added alongside.
- EV-DIA-007 | drawing-intake | finding | A rejecting batch also left the intake stranded in Processing forever, because the raw rejection escaped as a 422 extraction failure and never reached a Failed-state transition.
- EV-DIA-008 | drawing-intake | fix | assertBatch() wraps every child batch. It validates result exists AND result.success === true for every statement, and converts BOTH a rejection and a success:false result into one canonical DRAWING_INTAKE_PERSISTENCE_FAILED error.
- EV-DIA-009 | drawing-intake | fix | Success is asserted positively. A result with no success property is treated as UNPROVEN and fails closed, because absence of a failure marker is not evidence of success. This was proven: node:sqlite run() returns only changes,lastInsertRowid with no success field.
- EV-DIA-010 | drawing-intake | fix | Diagnostics carry statement index, target table, driver code and driver message. Bound values are never included, so drawing text cannot leak. Asserted by a test.
- EV-DIA-011 | drawing-intake | fix | verifyPersistedChildren() queries the canonical tables directly before Completed and compares persisted vs expected for pages, assets, search entries and legends. It also verifies project_id, document_id and document_version_id on the parent row.
- EV-DIA-012 | drawing-intake | fix | Extraction producing zero children is accepted only when it genuinely produced none. An expected>0 actual=0 mismatch fails closed with an explicit reason.
- EV-DIA-013 | drawing-intake | fix | A persistence failure marks the intake Failed via markIntakeFailed instead of leaving it Processing. Marking is best-effort and never masks the thrown error.
- EV-DIA-014 | drawing-intake | fix | Persistence failure returns HTTP 500 with code DRAWING_INTAKE_PERSISTENCE_FAILED, distinguished from extraction failure HTTP 422 DRAWING_INTAKE_FAILED. Never 201.
- EV-DIA-015 | drawing-intake | test | tests/drawing-intake-atomicity.test.mjs, 10/10 pass. Covers positive under both batch shapes, negative throwing, negative success:false, negative silent-drop, missing success flag, failure lifecycle, and document/version binding.
- EV-DIA-016 | drawing-intake | test | Mutation proven. Removing assertBatch wrapper => 4 failures. Removing post-write verification => 1 failure. Skipping only the search-entry child writes => 3 failures. Weakening success!==false to success===true => 1 failure after the new guard test. Dropping markIntakeFailed => 1 failure.
- EV-DIA-017 | drawing-intake | lesson | The test harness needed two corrections before it tested anything real: identity is SERVER-configured via APP_USER_ID/APP_ORGANIZATION_ID and request headers are deliberately ignored, and project_members needs status and revoked_at columns for resolveProjectAuthority. Passing identity headers produced a 404 that looked like a handler failure.
- EV-DIA-018 | drawing-intake | note | drawing-extraction-api (10 fail) and drawing-intake-engine (1 fail) fail because drizzle/ is entirely absent from the shared dirty tree, 81 migrations deleted by another lane. Neither imports the changed code path and neither reaches persist(). Pre-existing, not a regression from this change.

Deriving "the panel must be UL listed" from it would invent a requirement the project never made. Seq 100313's own link stays Confirmed and its body-only `UL` standard row stays in place, because `unfalsifiableStandardCitations` refuses body-only citations and therefore cannot manufacture a false pass either way. **THE GENUINE CLAUSE IS SEQ 100075** (`specjob_..._requirement_75`, p.5 clause P, Mandatory/Compliance, Approved, `approved_for_downstream=1`, **0 live links**): "Every component of the fire alarm system shall be listed under a single manufacturer, approved by Underwriters Laboratories (UL), and clearly bear the UL certification." Its parent clause explicitly names "The fire alarm control panel, repeater panel, and Building Fire Alarm Panel". It is deliberately DISCLOSED, NOT rewritten. **A HEDGED DEMAND DELIBERATELY DERIVES NOTHING.** The modal gate admits only an obligation ("shall", "must", "is required to", "needs to"). "Detectors should be listed by UL where available" derives nothing, because `modality()` classifies a sentence with no recognised soft modal as Mandatory and admitting it would turn the project's own hedge into a BLOCKING listing gate. **SEVEN NEGATIVE CONTROLS PLUS THREE MORE, ALL PASSING (24/24 in `tests/listing-authority-requirement.test.mjs`).** (A) UL requirement + current governed UL listing -> satisfied `Verified Listed`. (B) UL requirement + only FM approval -> `Evidence Missing`, blocking. (C) UL 864 requirement + generic UL listing -> numbered gate still blocks. (D) UL requirement + exact UL 864 certification -> listing satisfied AND the requirement object still carries no number. (E) unrelated SKU / variant-scoped certification -> not accepted. (F) superseded / deleted / Rejected / Needs Review -> not accepted. (F2) an APPROVED row recorded as "Not Listed"/"Withdrawn"/"Revoked"/"Expired" -> `Listing Contradicted`, blocking; an UNAPPROVED such row is NEITHER proof NOR contradiction, or anyone could block a product by filing junk. (G) no listing requirement -> a candidate carrying rich certifications scores exactly the same `standardsEvidence`, proving certifications never raise compliance on their own; (G2) an UNSATISFIED requirement scores 0, never a free pass. **THE CONFIDENCE EXTENSION IS MINIMAL AND FAILS CLOSED.** Only `standardsEvidence` changed: `evidenceRows = [...standardRows, ...listingRows]`, then the pre-existing fallback, with `profile?.listingRequirements?.length` added as a second "the project demands something" signal. A new critical component was deliberately NOT added, because a new component can only become a new minimum. `LISTING_ASSERTING_CERTIFICATION_TYPES` accepts Listing claim / Standard compliance / Approval claim and refuses Seismic claim (a seismic qualification is not a listing). A requirement whose listing claim was DECIDED is added to `structuredRequirementIds` by the same `governingRequirementId` contract `evaluateCapabilities` uses; without it the mandatory clause P would ALSO be reported "Missing Product Data" and block every product including a genuinely listed one — proven by test. | 2026-10-03 | `tests/listing-authority-requirement.test.mjs`; `package.json` (`test:phase3`) | Invalidated by inventing a second listing-body allowlist instead of deriving from `STANDARD_BODIES.productListingBody`; by letting the derivation fire on a listing word that qualifies a following noun; by admitting a hedged "should ... where available"; by reading a number off the product and writing it onto the requirement; by making `Certification Evidence Not Approved` a pass; by letting an unapproved row manufacture a contradiction; by folding listing requirements into `profile.standards`; or by relaxing `unfalsifiableStandardCitations`. It produced NO suggestion for seq 100075. Cause, measured by calling `scoreRequirementLink` directly: score **8**, evidence `["Same engineering system (+8)", "Equipment type unresolved (+0)"]`, `requirementEquipment = Unknown`. Three independent reasons, each structural rather than incidental: (1) `looseFireAlarmEquipmentMatch` fails closed on clause text that names THREE panel types ("fire alarm control panel, repeater panel, and Building Fire Alarm Panel"); (2) `panel` is in `NOT_DISTINCTIVE_BARE_FORMS`, so the loose pass can never recover it; (3) `genericLinkTerms` contains `alarm`, `control` and `panel`, so EVERY distinctive term of the panel's own description is filtered out of the technical-match term set. The threshold is `confidence >= 25 || (mandatory && >= 15)`, so 8 can never surface. **THE GENERAL FORM OF THE GAP: a mandatory compliance clause that names no equipment and shares no distinctive technical term with the BOQ row can never be proposed for applicability, at any score.** That is not specific to seq 100075 and not specific to this panel. **ROUTE 2 — `POST /api/requirements/:id/propagate-system-wide`.** Refused on its own gate: `SYSTEM_WIDE_REQUIREMENT_PATTERN` is `/\bentire\s+(?:\w+\s+){0,4}system\b/i`, which requires the word "entire"; seq 100075 says "Every component of the fire alarm system", so `isSystemWideRequirementText` is `false` and `allSystemCategories: true` returns `REQUIREMENT_NOT_SYSTEM_WIDE_SCOPED`. The route's explicit engineer-classified `categories` mode cannot reach the panel either, because it gates on `currentApprovedUnderstandingFacts`, and the panel's BOQ understanding review status is **`null`** — it has no `estimator_understanding_review_versions` row at all, so the route skips it. Project-wide there are 108 current BOQ items, of which 29 are `APPROVED` (Detection Devices 8, Control Equipment 9, Manual Initiation 4, Modules and Interfaces 8) and **the panel is not among them.** **ROUTE 3 — `POST /api/requirement-links/:id/confirm`.** Structurally dependent on Route 1: it only ever UPDATEs a current row and is guarded to `Suggested`/`Needs Review`, so it cannot create the first link. **WHY NO SCORER CHANGE WAS MADE.** The one-line fix would be to stop filtering the panel's own distinctive terms, or to widen `SYSTEM_WIDE_REQUIREMENT_PATTERN`. Both change behaviour for every item in the project — 108 current items, 2037 confirmed links, 853 consolidated requirements, 217 profiles, 2230 intelligence facts — and would need its own governance review and its own non-regression baseline. It is not a special case for seq 100075 and it is not this lane's mandate, and `scoreRequirementLink` is explicitly documented as "a suggestion heuristic feeding human review, never authority". **THE GATE WAS LEFT CLOSED.** `standardsEvidence` is still 0 on the live decision: `evidenceRows` is empty (no listing requirement is linked) and `profile.standards.length > 0` (the body-only `UL` row from seq 313), so the fail-closed fallback returns 0. `confidenceLevel(0) = Unknown`, `overall = MIN(critical components)`, and the governed approve route requires `/^Eligible/`. **NOTHING WAS FABRICATED: `safety_approval_requests` is 0 rows DB-wide.** Next smallest step is a governed decision on the applicability scorer gap (a separate review), after which this model applies unchanged

- EV-20261003-COMPETITIVE-ENGINEER-UX-BENCHMARK | Benchmark research into how mature fire-alarm/estimating/takeoff tools structure the engineer workflow, applied to derive a target engineer IA for Al Mousa | Honeywell ESSER SpecBuilder + CLSS Tool Suite + XLS3000 datasheet; Simplex FQQ Sales and Design Tool; ConEst SureCount + IntelliBid; Autodesk Forma Takeoff; Bluebeam Revu Quantity Link; FireCAD/Inspect Point; Apollo + Eaton loop design calculators | **Patterns that are industry-common (evidence-backed).** (1) Exception-first: FireCAD "performs live error checking as you draft -- flagging missing devices, addressable loop conflicts, and wiring violations before they make it to submittal"; SpecBuilder validates "loads and capacities ... in real time". So validity is computed continuously, not at a review gate. (2) Readiness state carries a plain-language reason: FQQ ships a "FQECM -- Quick Error Checking Matrix for Rapid Troubleshooting"; our own `calculateReadiness` already emits `blockingReasons` in plain English. (3) Device-selection matrix organised by area/floor/phase, auto-tallied: FQQ uses "customizable columns headers, example: Floor 1, Floor 2, Floor 3" and "device selections are automatically tallied"; SureCount synchronises "drawings and phases" with estimating. (4) Panel capacity verified against device selection: FQQ "Verification of panel capacities versus device selection". (5) Spare capacity is an explicit designer parameter, separable from demand: FQQ lets users "add 'spare devices' without affecting loop requirement calculations" and sets "customizable spare capacity for addressable points"; industry guidance is explicit that "the maximum number of addresses a loop supports is a ceiling, not a design target". (6) Revision diff is a first-class view: SureCount Overlay shows "new items in green and items that were deleted from the original drawing in red"; Autodesk offers to "compare changes to newer models and drawings". (7) Quantity is never detached from source: Autodesk's unified workflow yields "a comprehensive quantity inventory that provides an audit trail, so teams can quickly understand where the summary quantities came from"; Bluebeam Quantity Link is a live BIDIRECTIONAL PDF<->sheet link so revision "no re-export, no manual reconciliation, no risk of the quantity data and the cost model drifting apart". (8) Scale is calibrated before measuring (Bluebeam prompts calibration on every page) and per-viewport scale is supported. (9) Hierarchy panel -> circuit/loop -> device: FQQ offers a "Circuit Tree View with Easily Identifiable Tap Levels". (10) Documentation is generated FROM the model: FQQ one-line riser, FireCAD risers/schedules/reports, SpecBuilder export. (11) Prior work is editable without restart: FQQ "editing of previously entered items without need for restart". **Fire-alarm-specific and directly load-bearing for us.** Honeywell XLS3000: "Up to 159 detectors and 159 modules per SLC" -- detectors and modules are SEPARATE pools, both consuming SLC addresses. Modules driving CONVENTIONAL devices consume one address for many physical devices. This is the industry basis for our physical-quantity vs SLC-address-demand distinction and for the Fireman Telephone Jack case; our own `SLC_RESOURCE_STATES` carries `SLC_ROLE_ESTABLISHED` with the comment "the number of SLC addresses a unit consumes is not evidenced anywhere in this repository. Carries a null demand on purpose" -- correct fail-closed behaviour, and exactly the state a UI must be able to SHOW. FQQ also selects device+accessory as one unit ("Smoke Detector with Standard Base"), matching our BOM accessory model, and provides a "Third Party Items tab" for non-vendor products, matching our manufacturer-neutral knowledge base. **Product-specific, explicitly NOT copied.** FQQ's Excel-365 foundation and its Bluebeam-CSV-to-Solution-Navigator pricing bridge are a vendor commercial ecosystem; SureCount's "patented auto count"; FQQ IDNAC/T-Tap/Class A-B circuit engineering; FireCAD NFPA coverage-radius placement. **Critical grounding for our target model:** `calculateReadiness` in app/domain/technical-requirement-engine.mjs already computes 8 governed statuses in strict severity order -- Classification Required -> Conflict Blocking -> Missing Critical Information -> Needs Technical Review -> Ready with Warnings -> Ready for Matching -- each with human-readable `blockingReasons` (e.g. "No confirmed mandatory technical baseline exists."). `PROJECT_SLC_SIZING_STATUSES` is only ["AUTHORITATIVE_PANEL_SIZING","INSUFFICIENT_TOPOLOGY_EVIDENCE"], so a truthful NOT-READY sizing state already exists. `ALLOCATION_STATUSES` = FULLY/PARTIALLY_ALLOCATED, AMBIGUOUS, UNALLOCATED. Therefore the Needs Attention model is a VIEW over already-computed governed state, not a new framework -- the UX gap is that none of this vocabulary is rendered. | 2026-10-03 | `app/domain/technical-requirement-engine.mjs:417-424,69-70`; `app/domain/fire-alarm-slc-resource-classifier.mjs:26-37`; `app/domain/fire-alarm-panel-demand-allocation.mjs:7-12`; `app/domain/fire-alarm-panel-slc-sizing.mjs:15-18`; vendor docs listed above | Superseded if our readiness vocabulary changes; benchmark claims re-verifiable at the cited vendor pages | VALID. - EV-20261003-SHARED-SBUS-LOOP-TOPOLOGY-CLOSED | The 63-expander / 64-loop figure is a valid ABSOLUTE PRODUCT TOPOLOGY bound, conditional on zero other SBUS devices, and it does NOT conflict with the 2100-point ceiling because the two constrain different things | Honeywell 6815 SLC Expander datasheet + 351602 datasheet, both text-extractable under `out/vendor-sources/honeywell/ifp2100hv/` | **The apparent contradiction is resolved: 64 is real, and it bounds a different quantity than 2100.** Three first-party statements, quoted exactly: (1) 351602 SYSTEM CAPACITY -- "**SBUS devices: 63 any combination**"; (2) 6815 datasheet -- "The number of 6815's used is **limited by the number of SBUS devices**"; (3) 6815 datasheet -- "IFP2100/ECS FACP **supports 63 6815s** but a maximum of 2100 SLC devices per system". Read together these fix the resource model: the panel holds a **63-device SBUS budget**, 6815s draw from that budget one-for-one (63 x 6815 against a 63-device pool is the explicit statement of that 1:1 mapping), and 63 is therefore the ABSOLUTE number of expanders reachable when 6815s are the ONLY SBUS consumers. Since a 6815 adds exactly one SLC loop and the panel has one native loop, **ABSOLUTE_PRODUCT_MAX_SLC_LOOPS = 1 + 63 = 64**, under the explicit condition that no other SBUS device consumes the budget. **64 x 318 = 20,352 does NOT invalidate it.** That product is meaningless as a capacity figure: the 2100 ceiling bounds POPULATED DEMAND, while loop topology bounds INSTALLABLE LOOPS. Deriving system capacity from `max loops x 318` is exactly the forbidden operation, and 64 must never be multiplied out. `SLOTS_CONSUMED_PER_6815 = 1` is implied by (1)+(2)+(3) rather than literally stated, and is recorded as implied. `SBUS_DEVICE_CAPACITY = 63` is scoped **per FACP** -- not per cabinet (a cabinet houses at most two 6815s) and not per network (networking is a separate 32-panel figure). **SBUS pool consumers (verbatim from 351602 COMPATIBLE SBUS DEVICES):** RA-2000 / RA-2000GRAY 4x40 LCD annunciator; RA-1000 / RA-1000R / RA-100 4x20 LCD annunciator; 5865-3 LED annunciator (30 LEDs); 5865-4 LED annunciator with silence/reset keys; 5880 LED I/O module (40 LED outputs + 8 supervised dry contacts); 5883 relay interface (10 Form C); 5824 serial/parallel printer interface; plus 6815 (SLC expander), 5815XL (SD SLC expander), and the SK-NIC / SK-FML / SK-FSL network and fiber modules. Every one of these draws on the same 63-device budget, which is what "any combination" means. 351602 also states "**Note: SK and SD devices cannot be mixed in the same fire alarm system**", independently corroborating the IDP/SK branch. **OCR WAS NOT REQUIRED** -- these three statements fully answer the shared-SBUS and topology question, so LS10143-001SK-E was not OCR'd. | 2026-10-03 | `hbt-fire-6815_Datasheet.pdf`, `hbt-fire-351602-C.pdf` | Invalidated if Honeywell revises the SBUS budget or the 6815 addressing model | VALID. Supersedes the "MAX_SUPPORTED_SLC_LOOPS = UNRESOLVED" position of EV-20261003-SLC-LOOP-SEMANTICS-CORRECTION: it is now resolved as an absolute topology bound of 64, while remaining occupancy-dependent in practice
- EV-20261003-SIZING-ENGINE-NEEDS-NO-MAX-LOOP-SCALAR | The SLC sizing engine consumes NO maximum-loop input; `max_slc_loops` is demanded only by a readiness gate that cannot use it safely, so the correct model is requirement-relative sufficiency, not an absolute scalar | `app/domain/fire-alarm-slc-capacity-calculator.mjs`; `app/domain/fire-alarm-panel-capability-normalization.mjs:712` | **Verified by inspection, not inference.** `calculateSlcExpansion` accepts exactly five capacity inputs -- `nativeLoops`, `detectorsPerLoop`, `modulesPerLoop`, `systemPointCeiling`, and the optional `maxTotalPointsPerLoop`. Grep for `max_slc_loops|maxSlcLoops|maxLoops|loopCeiling` across the calculator, `fire-alarm-panel-slc-sizing.mjs` and `fire-alarm-panel-sizing-snapshot.mjs` returns **nothing**: the engine never bounds total loops and never multiplies loops by per-loop capacity. Its own system-capacity guard is `totalPointsDemand > systemPointCeiling`, which is independent and already correct. **`MAX_SLC_LOOPS` appears in exactly two places, both in `fire-alarm-panel-capability-normalization.mjs`** -- the scope declaration and `assessSizingReadiness`'s `required` array (line 712). So it is a **readiness-gate artifact, never a calculation input**: the model currently blocks on a fact the engine does not use, and which cannot be used safely because its value is occupancy-dependent. **The real gap is different from the assumed one.** The engine is not missing a loop ceiling; it is missing proof that the required additional loops are OBTAINABLE. It will compute `requiredAdditionalLoops` and demand that many expansion units with no check against available SBUS slots, enclosure capacity, or the point ceiling's own loop implication. Recommended input model: an `expansionAvailability` block carrying `supportedAtLeastLoops` (a LOWER BOUND), `availableExpanderSlots`, and `slotsPerExpander`, evaluated REQUIREMENT-RELATIVE -- sizing may proceed when `available expansion loops >= required additional loops`. This is sound precisely because the 2100 ceiling caps usable loops at 7 while the absolute topology bound is 64, so the project-effective ceiling is the MINIMUM of the point ceiling, available SBUS slots and enclosure capacity -- never a single stored scalar. A `PROVEN_MINIMUM_AVAILABLE_LOOPS` / `SUPPORTED_AT_LEAST_N_LOOPS` lower-bound primitive is the correct representation, since an absolute maximum is both occupancy-dependent and, in practice, never the binding constraint. | 2026-10-03 | `app/domain/fire-alarm-slc-capacity-calculator.mjs`; `app/domain/fire-alarm-panel-capability-normalization.mjs:712` | Invalidated if the engine gains a loop-ceiling input or if occupancy ever becomes project-governed state | VALID. Read-only inspection. No calculator or readiness code was modified in this slice

With a null observation instead of an explicit empty one it did the same at CONFIDENCE 50. A cleaner null signal produced a MORE confident fabrication, so this is not a prompt-wording accident.

The project already models this deterministically (legend symbol geometry).
