- EV-20261003-SEVEN-IS-NOT-A-LOOP-CEILING | CORRECTION: "the point ceiling caps usable loops at 7" is false. 7 is the loop count needed to reach the 2100 ceiling with BOTH POOLS FULLY LOADED, not a maximum. Ten lightly-loaded loops under 2100 total demand are valid | `app/domain/fire-alarm-slc-capacity-calculator.mjs`; `app/domain/fire-alarm-panel-capability-normalization.mjs`; `tests/slc-capacity-foundation.test.mjs` | This is the SECOND time one constraint has been collapsed into another and then corrected: first `CEILING(2100/318)=7` was promoted to MAX_SUPPORTED_SLC_LOOPS, then the derived 7 was restated as a ceiling on loop count. Both were wrong in the same way -- treating a derived arithmetic artifact as if it were a manufacturer constraint. `CEILING(2100/318)=7` holds only when both pools are FULLY loaded (159 detectors AND 159 modules on every loop). A design of 10 loops carrying 1500 detectors and 60 modules totals 1560 points, is comfortably under 2100, respects both per-loop pools, and is a perfectly valid system. **Three constraints remain strictly independent and none is derived from another:** (1) PER-LOOP RESOURCE -- detectors <= 159 AND modules <= 159, with `requiredLoops = MAX(CEILING(detectors/159), CEILING(modules/159))`; (2) SYSTEM-WIDE POINT -- detectors + modules <= 2100, absolute and independent of how the demand is spread; (3) LOOP TOPOLOGY / SHARED RESOURCES -- whether the required loop count is OBTAINABLE from expansion architecture and the shared 63-device SBUS budget. A regression control now pins this: 10 loops, 1590 total points, 10 governed expansion loops -> expansion PASS and ceiling PASS, i.e. a result above 7 that is fully valid.
- EV-20261003-REQUIREMENT-RELATIVE-LOOP-FEASIBILITY-IMPLEMENTED | MAX_SLC_LOOPS removed as a universal readiness gate; two independent tri-state feasibility axes added to the SLC engine; loop topology decided requirement-relatively | `app/domain/fire-alarm-slc-capacity-calculator.mjs`; `app/domain/fire-alarm-panel-capability-normalization.mjs`; `tests/slc-capacity-foundation.test.mjs` | **Readiness gate corrected.** `assessSizingReadiness` no longer lists MAX_SLC_LOOPS in `required`; it is reported in a new `advisory` array and the return now carries `loopTopologyAuthority: "REQUIREMENT_RELATIVE"` plus an explanatory note. `required` is now `[BASE_SLC_LOOPS, PER_LOOP_DETECTORS, PER_LOOP_MODULES, SYSTEM_POINT_CEILING]` -- SYSTEM_POINT_CEILING was deliberately NOT relaxed. MAX_SLC_LOOPS was blocking on a fact the engine never reads and cannot read safely, because the real ceiling is occupancy-dependent. **Two independent tri-state axes (`PASS`/`FAIL`/`UNKNOWN`) added to engine output:** `expansionFeasibility` from `expansionAvailability { supportedAtLeastTotalLoops | availableExpanderSlots + slotsPerExpander | availableMountingKits + expandersPerMountingKit }`, combined with MINIMUM because they are independent bounds; and `systemPointFeasibility` from demand vs the 2100 ceiling. Also emitted: `requiredLoops · nativeLoops · requiredAdditionalLoops · availableExpansionLoops · supportedTotalLoops · systemPointCeiling · systemPointDemand · expansionBounds`. A new status `EXPANSION_TOPOLOGY_INSUFFICIENT` distinguishes "the arithmetic is sound but the loops cannot be OBTAINED" from plain `EXPANSION_REQUIRED` ("expansion is needed"). **Fail-closed on shared resources:** with no `expansionAvailability` the result is `UNKNOWN`, never an assumption that all 63 SBUS slots are free. **Mounting is a bound, never a global ceiling:** `availableMountingKits x expandersPerMountingKit` enters the MINIMUM, and a `localPanelMountingCapacity` field is carried but never used as a system bound -- verified by a control where 2 remote kits x 2 cards allow 4 expansion loops, exceeding the cabinet's own 2. **Legacy inert:** a stale `max_slc_loops: 3` on `panelCapacity` is verified not to govern a 4-loop requirement. **One behaviour surfaced rather than left ambiguous:** when the ABSOLUTE point ceiling fails, loop arithmetic is short-circuited so no expansion requirement exists and `expansionFeasibility` is UNKNOWN. A new `expansionFeasibilityNote` states this is "NOT EVALUATED: the system point ceiling already fails", explicitly distinguished from missing SBUS evidence so it cannot be misread. - EV-GB-001 | authority | finding | FAIL-OPEN ROOT CAUSE: acceptance authority sat inside the model. With a BLANK image the previous run produced FIREMAN TELEPHONE JACK / CONFIDENCE 100 / CONSISTENT / ACCEPT_FACT while Stage 1 had correctly reported GLYPH_COUNT=0, ENCLOSURE_SHAPE=NONE, OBSERVATION_CONFIDENCE=0. The model discarded a perfect null signal and answered from the legend alone. Prompt wording cannot fix this because the model is authority over its own output and confidence is not evidence. Acceptance authority was therefore moved OUT of the model into a deterministic gate (scripts/benchmark-12-evidence-gate.mjs).
- EV-GB-002 | authority | finding | THE GATE TAKES NO CONFIDENCE INPUT AT ALL, and applyGate is MONOTONICALLY DOWNWARD: it can turn ACCEPT_FACT into KEEP_UNKNOWN but never the reverse. MODEL_CONFIDENCE_IS_NOT_AUTHORITY is structural, not a prompt instruction.
- EV-GB-003 | authority | correction | A REAL INVERTED-LOGIC BUG in the first version of my own gate, found by replay rather than by the adversarial suite. The line `if (eligibleForReasoning && reasons.length) eligibleForAcceptance = false;` meant that when eligibleForReasoning was FALSE (a hard block) the condition was false and ELIGIBLE_FOR_ACCEPTANCE stayed TRUE. The blank-image replay then returned FIREMAN TELEPHONE JACK / ACCEPT_FACT / c100 / CONSISTENT with overrode=false. Fixed by splitting into two EXPLICIT lists (reasoningBlocks, acceptanceBlocks) with eligibleForAcceptance = eligibleForReasoning && acceptanceBlocks.length===0. After the fix the same replay is overridden to UNKNOWN / KEEP_UNKNOWN / c0 / INSUFFICIENT. LESSON: an adversarial suite that always routes blocked cases through forcedBlockedResult instead of applyGate CANNOT detect this class of bug; the override path must be exercised explicitly.
- EV-GB-004 | infra | finding | The decisive discriminator between legend (S) SMOKE DETECTOR and schedule [S]C is the ENCLOSURE SHAPE, which never appears in the text layer. That is why a text-only reasoner could not reach it and why a VLM was being asked to do a job the application can do deterministically.
- EV-GB-005 | infra | finding | DETERMINISTIC ENCLOSURE CLASSIFIER, 7/7 on real Al Mousa schedule cells. Primary discriminator is the longest straight ink run relative to cell size: a square draws long straight edges, a circle has none. Measured on identical 28x22pt cells at 6x: BOX enclosures gave longestRun 71-79px (runRatio 0.53-0.60), ROUND enclosures gave 28px (runRatio 0.21). Clean wide separation, no threshold sensitivity. Earlier attempts using corner/edge-midpoint ink DENSITY bands were unreliable (3/7) because bands near the centre sample the LETTER inside the symbol rather than its outline, and because a riser line enters every plan symbol from above and inks the top edge.
- EV-GB-006 | infra | finding | Region type is now DERIVED deterministically from tokens INSIDE the target region only. Using the wider row neighbourhood previously made a GRS PLAN symbol inherit the SCHEDULE row printed above it, reproducing exactly the legend-vs-schedule confusion the classifier exists to prevent. Correct classification on this run: T=PLAN, S+C/S H/HC=SCHEDULE.
- EV-GB-007 | infra | finding | Honesty boundary: the geometry packet is a RASTER proxy measured from rendered ink structure, NOT true PDF vector-operator parsing. It is deterministic and reproducible but it is a measurement, never authority.

SQLite does not implicitly wrap DDL, so on a populated database the `DROP TABLE` + `ALTER TABLE ... RENAME` after a FAILED copy-in still executed: measured, `profile_requirement_applicability` went 1,121 rows -> **0 rows, present but EMPTY**, with both authority columns gone. A loud abort had become silent data loss, and the pipeline's exit status was the only signal -- arriving after the damage. **ALSO: SHELL PIPE SUCCESS SEMANTICS ARE NOT A SAFETY MECHANISM** and were removed entirely. The executor runs every statement through ONE `DatabaseSync` connection inside one transaction, so the transaction is genuinely shared (a pipe cannot guarantee this), takes the write lock up front with `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` so a concurrent writer cannot interleave, and keeps a file-level `.pre-migration.bak` for the hard-crash case that rollback cannot cover. **PROVED BY AN INTENTIONAL FAILURE, NOT BY INSPECTION** (`scripts/prove-migration-atomicity.sh`): a 50-row table, a 5-statement migration aborting at statement 2, result is exit 1 with rows 50 -> 50, table shape byte-identical, **zero `__new_` residue, and the post-failure index absent**. This is not a weakening: Drizzle's own rebuild idiom cannot run with enforcement ON, 0019's header documents the same requirement, and `foreign_key_check` is re-run afterwards by the validation harness -- canonical D1 has zero FK violations before and after | 2026-10-03 | `bash scripts/prove-migration-atomicity.sh` -> ATOMICITY PROVEN; the earlier `NOT NULL constraint failed` mid-migration abort that discarded 18 statements | Invalidated if the chain's execution semantics change.
